# STATUS_HISTORY.md — the compiler's running status: every landing's paragraph, newest first, and the cycle history

Moved VERBATIM out of `CLAUDE.md` at landing 157 (the author's decision, Q70, 2026-10-23: `CLAUDE.md` had grown to 839 KB and
was loaded into every turn of every session). Below the rule is the old `## Status` section byte for byte as it stood at landing
156 (`a5693a6`). A landing's head paragraph is inserted at the top of it, as the landings always did (the apply scripts' anchor
is the previous landing's head opening, `IS UNDERWAY: **…`); `CLAUDE.md` keeps a short status that points here.

`dyn`, `any`, an enum with payloads, an unresolved type and fuel run out answer yes -- the closed direction, DEF-54; a struct, an `Optional`, a `Result`, an array, a slice or a `List` when a member does; a scalar or a string no; a pointer TO the `List` is D-286's plain party); `prescan_party` pushes one party per address (`pre_has_elem_party`) and marks a pointer root (`root_ident_is_pointer`); `acc_call`'s claim loop pushes a party of the `List` FOR THE CALL for an element-address argument (holder 0, truncated at the call's end), so the other arguments' summaries meet it; A SPAWN'S LEND, AND A VIEW OF A POINTEE, WERE COMPARED AGAINST A WRITE ONLY WHERE THE WRITE SHARED THE CROSSING'S OR THE VIEW'S OWN ROOT, never where a POINTER reached the same storage by another name (DEF-306, HIGH, registered 2026-10-24 by `nitpick-compiler_48` at landing 161; DEF-308 its view twin): a spawn LENDS its crossing until the block's join (`NITPICK-BORROW-016`, D-180), tracked by the ROOT symbol handed to the spawn (`spawn_lent.npk`, DEF-148, is the same-name case — lend `@s`, write `s`), so `shared_arena<int64>:s = shared_arena_make(8i64); shared_arena<int64>->:sp = @s; drop filler(@s); drop replace(sp);` (`replace` doing `(<-p) = shared_arena_make(4i64)`) dropped the old arena — its storage freed — while the spawned `filler` task still held `@s`, and the reverse (`drop filler(sp); s = shared_arena_make(4i64);`) replaced the crossing by its own name under a lend THROUGH the pointer: a use-after-free / data race in safe code at both legs (exit 107/113 or a `WildLeak`), on every compiler to date; and for the view domain (DEF-308), with `fixed uint8[]:vw = string_bytes(h.o);` live, `drop wr(hp);` — `wr` doing `(<-p) = H2{ … }` — compiled and read 113 (the freed bytes reused, where 97 was stored) at both legs. DEF-302 (landing 160) closed a DIRECT whole-pointee write through a named pointer (`(<-hp) = H2{ … }`, `move(<-hp)`) against a view by another name; the faces left open were a CALLEE that replaces the whole pointee, handed a named pointer, and the whole LEND domain. MEASURED FIRST on landing 162's compiler by a MATRIX over the axes holder × side × write × pointer-path (`.internal/probes/def306/matrix306.py`, kept as a tool at `.internal/def306_matrix_kept/`), each cell REFUSED, a named control, or N/A, the check exiting non-zero on any open cell: under the fix the matrix exits 0 (405 cells — 158 REFUSED, 5 KNOWN-LIMIT citing DEF-309/DEF-310, 0 OPEN, 242 N/A; 13/13 controls; the self-test proving both fail-closed exits), under landing 162's compiler it exits 1 (70 un-listed open cells, the live exit-(i) control). The five genuinely open cells were SILENT on landing 162 too (pre-existing, not regressions). THE CAUSE (alias.npk): `check_access_reported`'s rootless gate judged a write by the written place's root, and a whole-pointee write through a NAMED pointer handed to a callee (`wr(hp)`, `(<-p) = …`) fell to that gate and was judged by NOTHING — the callee holds the pointer's VALUE and cannot reassign the caller's binding, so its `(<-p) = …` is a write of what the pointer points at, which no reader resolved; and `lend_spawn_args` rooted a spawn's lend at the pointer BINDING (`root_ptr`) or, for a call's result or a field, NOWHERE, so a crossing handed by a pointer and then reassigned, destroyed or moved by another name met no lend. NOW, ONE POINTER-RESOLUTION FEEDS BOTH CHECKS, reusing DEF-302's and DEF-305's resolvers. `ptr_place_written_whole` answers whether `place` is a NAMED pointer place that the written path `ap` writes WHOLE (pointer-typed, rooted at a name, `ap` its own steps and nothing below). `check_pointer_targets` gains a whole-pointee route: under `CHECK_VIEWS` or `CHECK_LENT` (a callee's summary) a whole-pointee write through a named pointer routes to `pe = place`, the pointer re-rooted at the storage it points at and checked against the VIEW and LENT parties there (`check_view_parties_at`); the place's OWN pointer is the write's, never an outer one (`ptr_last_base` answers `pap` for `pap.p`), and a bare pointer name written WHOLE under `CHECK_ALL` stays its reassignment, which moves the pointer and leaves the pointee alone (D-286, DEF-272). `lend_through_pointer` (in `lend_spawn_args`) resolves a spawn's pointer argument to its target(s): an `@place` by `ptr_last_base` + `pointer_targets_of`, a NAMED pointer value by `pointer_targets_of`, a CALL'S RESULT by `escape_pointer_targets` (D-326 rule A, DEF-305), pushing a LENT party per target beside the party `lend_spawn_args` roots at the pointer itself; an `@place` through a pointer lends each target WHOLE, and the spawned callee is read against the lend for what it writes through a pointer value (a task that stores over what it was lent). A pointer PARAMETER has no target here (`pointer_targets_of` reads a local's chain): its pointee is the caller's, and its lend is the pointer's. `NITPICK-BORROW-016` at the write, the call, or the spawn; `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the call for the view. Before it: **DEF-304 LANDED 2026-10-26 — A PLAIN POINTER INTO A `List` ELEMENT FREEZES THE `List` AGAINST A REALLOCATING OR DROPPING OPERATION, AS D-325 FREEZES A VIEW'S ROOT** (`PARTY_ELEM_ADDR`, a VIEW-like party in `check_access_reported`: `H2->:hp = @l[0i64];` then `list_push` past `cap`, `list_reserve`, `list_truncate`, `list_pop`, `list_clear`, a reassignment of the `List`, a move out of it or a `List` field grown, with the address live, is `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the operation; `place_is_list_element` recognises the element, the kind is judged by the mutation-summary path exactly as the view party is): A USE-AFTER-FREE IN SAFE CODE CLOSED IN EIGHT SPELLINGS, ONE BY-SCOPE FALSE POSITIVE ACCEPTED, OPTION (a) FREEZE (the author's Q75). Built, chained, recorded and landed by nitpick-compiler_48 (Opus) in wt/48g, branch 48-def304, on landing 161's commit (249eae9). A PLAIN POINTER INTO A `List` ELEMENT HELD ACROSS AN OPERATION THAT MOVES OR FREES THE BACKING BUFFER WAS JUDGED BY NOTHING (DEF-304, registered 2026-10-24 by `nitpick-compiler_48` at landing 160; HIGH: a use-after-free in safe code at both legs, on every compiler to date): with `List<H2>:l` holding one element and `H2->:hp = @l[0i64];` live, `list_push(@l, …)` past `cap` (the buffer grows) compiled and read the FREED buffer at both legs -- the pointer reads where the old buffer was, a negative or zero length (exit 10/11). A VIEW of the element (`string_bytes(l[0i64].o)`, the twin k304a) was refused already (BORROW-015, D-325: a view's root is frozen while the view is live); a plain address into a `List` element was judged by NOTHING -- D-286's plain-address rule, where `@` claims nothing and the conflict table names claims and held `@`s, never a plain element address of managed storage that moves. THE AUTHOR'S RULING, Q75 (≈19:55 2026-10-24 via management_3): option (a) FREEZE -- freeze the `List` while a plain pointer into one of its elements is live, do NOT refuse the pointer. MEASURED FIRST on landing 161's compiler (`wt/48a/.internal/probes/m2/u304*`, `results_u304_159.txt`): eight hazard shapes compiled and read the freed buffer at both legs -- `u304a` (the buffer grown, 11/11), `u304c` (reassigned, 11/11), `u304d` (truncated, 10/10), `u304e` (a field grown, 11/11), `u304f` (reserved, 11/11), `u304g` (popped, 10/10), `u304k` (moved out and dropped, 11/11), `u304l` (cleared, 10/10) -- while the two controls answered right: `u304h` (a pointer into a fixed ARRAY element across a reassignment, 0/0 -- an array's storage does not move) and `u304i` (a `$$i`/`$$m` claim held across a growth, already `NITPICK-BORROW-013`). The by-scope false positive `k304b` (the pointer dead before the growth but still in scope) and the two return/callee-provenance faces `u304j` (a call-result element pointer, 11/11) and `u304b` (an element pointer carried into a callee that reallocates through a pointer parameter, 11/11) were confirmed on THIS compiler's checker to be unmoved (the new checker reports no diagnostic on u304j or u304b) and are split off -- k304b accepted, u304j and u304b registered DEF-307. THE CAUSE (alias.npk): `check_access_reported` compared a reallocating or dropping access of a `List` against the live parties of its root, and a plain `@` address was a `PARTY_PLAIN` party, which `conflicts` treats as conflicting with a `$$`-claim alone -- never with a write-capable access -- so a `List` grown, shrunk, reassigned, moved or dropped under a live `@l[i]` met no conflict. NOW: A PLAIN ADDRESS INTO A `List` ELEMENT IS A VIEW-LIKE PARTY. A new party kind `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` (= 5, after `PARTY_LENT` = 4), raised by `party_kind_of` where an `@place` addresses a `List` element: `place_is_list_element` walks the addressed place (a `BORROW_FUEL` loop, counted) and answers true at an index whose base is a `List` (`type_is_list` on the base's type, `use "../type_layout.npk".*;` added for it), descending through a member access, a safe-nav and a deref, and answers false for a fixed array or a slice (whose storage does not move -- the control u304h). The kind is VIEW-LIKE: `conflicts` places it in the VIEW/SHARED/LENT group (conflicting with `WRITE|EXCLUSIVE`), `summary_judged` includes it, and `check_access_reported` routes it -- before the `PARTY_VIEW` arm -- to `report_elem_addr_conflict` (modelled on `report_view_conflict`, code `BORROW-015` the view's, a tailored sentence). So it is judged by the MUTATION-SUMMARY path (`check_written` -> `CHECK_VIEWS`) exactly as the view party is: a reallocating operation on `l` is a write-capable access of `l`'s storage, and the live `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` rooted at `l` conflicts with it; `l[i]` canonicalises to `l.items[…]` (D-314 §2), so the party overlaps the operation's write of `l.items`. `party_word` gains its arm; five `PARTY_PLAIN` sites (the non-local assign, the named-root `admit_value`, the `acc_call` claim loop, the ordinary-arg loop and the final claim recheck) guard `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` like `PLAIN`; `prescan_party` pushes it with `root_ptr:false` and `register_holder` makes it live for all kinds, so the pointer-exemption never applies. DESIGN REFINEMENT (recorded against the plan `wt/48d/.internal/t160/DEF304_PLAN.md`): the plan sketched `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` as a STANDALONE kind, adding `ACC_SHARED` and relying on a blanket `admit_written` of `ACC_WRITE`; the build made it VIEW-LIKE and `summary_judged` instead -- more precise: it never fires on `@l` handed to a READ-ONLY callee (the callee's summary carries no write of `l`), it does not conflict with a `$$i`/`$$m` claim where a view would not (the claims keep D-286's same-root rule), and the claim-held hazard u304i stays caught by the claim's OWN party (BORROW-013). The `place_is_list_element` walk is a counted loop, so it needs no `decreases` (D-304). CONSERVATIVE ON PURPOSE, the one by-scope false positive accepted (k304b, site 9 of the rejection file): a pointer into a `List` element that is DEAD before the reallocating operation but still in lexical scope -- the by-scope party is live, so the operation is refused though the pointer is never read again; 0 tree sites, recorded under D-286 (narrow later with by-last-use liveness of the holder, as DEF-302's k302a). THE CAUSE (alias.npk, escape.npk): `check_access_reported` resolves a write's conflicts from the written place's ROOT symbol, and DEF-302's forward reader `check_pointer_targets` finds the pointer an access goes through as the last pointer-typed base on its PLACE CHAIN (`ptr_write_base`) -- both answer nothing for a pointer that is a call's result (`place_root_symbol` 0, no place chain), so the two call-result shapes reached neither DEF-302 reader. And DEF-297/DEF-301's slice reader `escape_slice_targets` reads a slice's storage off the refs `collect_view_refs` carries, and that walk stops at a pointer: a deref bails, and a member recurses into the pointer LOCAL and reads ITS chain (the address of the pointee), not what the pointee views -- so a slice reached through a pointer had its targets read from the wrong chain and the write was admitted. NOW, TWO MECHANISMS, BOTH INDEPENDENT OF M3 (the store-matrix destination path, DEF-298 and DEF-299). A CALL-RESULT POINTER IS RESOLVED BY ITS RETURN PROVENANCE (rule A, D-326 -- the pointer twin of DEF-301's slice-no-place reader): `escape_pointer_targets` (escape.npk, the twin of `escape_slice_targets`) answers the storage a pointer-valued expression points at as target `ViewRef`s -- `collect_view_refs` reads what the result borrows, and each ref that is a TARGET of this pointer (a `REF_ADDR` whose place IS the pointee type, a `REF_PASS` whose place is the pointer's own type, as `record_refs_through_indirection` filters a bound pointer's chain; the place-end-type answers too) is the storage it points at; a ref rooted nowhere (a `wild` pointer, an allocator's result) gives none. THE FORWARD READER'S CALL-RESULT ARM (`check_pointer_targets`): when `ptr_write_base` is none AND the place is rootless (`place_root_symbol` 0) and pointer-typed, resolve it by `escape_pointer_targets`, the whole write path `ap` composed onto each target's own steps (`ptr_target_steps`), checked against the view parties there (`check_view_parties_at`) and the reverse (`check_pointer_view_parties`); a bare NAMED pointer whose `ptr_write_base` is none (a whole-pointee write, `ap` empty, but rooted) keeps the base's reading -- its reassignment is no conflict these readers judge (D-286, DEF-272). `check_access_reported`'s rootless block routes a rootless pointer-typed WRITE or EXCLUSIVE access that is not a `$$`-claim (`mode != CHECK_CLAIMS`, the claims keep D-286's same-root rule) to that arm before the shared-holder case, so `wp(raw idp(@h))`'s and `(raw idp(@h)).set()`'s stores are seen; `acc_call` drops the `is_place_chain` precondition on its pointer-receiver branch (a call-result receiver resolves by rule A in the arm) and routes a POINTER ARGUMENT no place names through `check_written` -> the same arm. `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the call. AND THE POINTER RESOLVER COMPOSES WITH THE SLICE RESOLVER: `escape_slice_targets` appends `escape_slice_targets_through_pointer` (escape.npk), which finds the first POINTER-typed sub-expression inward from the slice base, resolves it to its pointee(s) (`escape_pointer_targets`) and reads each pointee's recorded chain for runs of the slice's element (`push_binding_refs`, `escape_ref_is_run_of`) -- `bp.sl` reads `b`'s view of the array its slice field holds, `<-tp` reads the slice local `t`'s view of the array it ranges; over-approximate, adds targets never removes, and fires only when a pointer sits on the base's path. `escape_slice_own_holder` resolves a pointer root to its SINGLE pointee and tests THAT pointee's type (a slice, or a one-view struct -- `escape_view_carriers`), so the slice's OWN view of the run it spans is skipped by its holder (the POINTEE, not the pointer) and writing an element through it frees none of its own views; the validation walk steps through a pointer deref as it steps a member or a range. `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the write. NOW: THE ALIASING WALK RESOLVES A NAMED POINTER'S TARGET(S) AND CHECKS BOTH DIRECTIONS. For a WRITE or an EXCLUSIVE access that is not a `$$`-claim (`mode != CHECK_CLAIMS`, keeping D-286's same-root rule for the claims), two readers run after the slice block. `pointer_targets_of` answers the storage a pointer place points at, as target `ViewRef`s read off the chain of the pointer's root (`place_root_symbol`): a bare pointer local or a pointer field of a LOCAL (`hp`, `o.inner`) takes its targets off the root's own chain (`hp = @h`, `o.inner = @h` both recorded `@h` there — a REF_ADDR whose place IS the pointee type, a REF_PASS whose place is the pointer's own type, `ptr_ref_is_target`), a pointer reached THROUGH another pointer (`bp.p`: `bp = @b`, `b.p = @h`) is resolved one hop at a time (the inner pointer `bp` to its pointees, `ptr_inner_base`, then each pointee's chain for this pointer's own targets), fuel-bounded (8) and over-approximate as `record_refs_through_indirection` is — ANY ref of the pointee type is a target, more targets never fewer; a pointer rooted at a parameter, a `wild` pointer or an allocator's result gives none (a pointer parameter's pointee is DEF-275's store-matrix question, D-315, D-361). THE FORWARD READER (`check_pointer_targets`): the pointer an access goes through is `ptr_write_base` — the place itself when the access continues past its own steps and it is a pointer (a bare `hp` handed a written path below it, a method's `self.o`), else the last pointer-typed base on the path (`ptr_last_base`: `hp.o`→`hp`, `o.inner.v`→`o.inner`, `bp.p.o`→`bp.p`); each target is re-rooted and the write's steps BELOW the pointer (`pre.count … ap.count`) composed onto the target's own steps (`ptr_target_steps`), so `hp.o` targets `h.o` and never the whole of `h` — a write to a DISJOINT field reads disjoint (the control `ctl_disjoint_fields`) — then checked against the view parties there (`check_view_parties_at`, DEF-297's reader) and the views taken through a pointer at the same target (the reverse reader); a bare pointer NAME written whole under `CHECK_ALL` is a reassignment (`hp = …`), left alone. THE REVERSE READER (`check_pointer_view_parties`): a write to a root by the pointee's own name, against each live VIEW party taken THROUGH a pointer whose resolved storage includes that root (`vw = string_bytes(hp.o)`, write `h.o`; the two-level `bp.p.o`), the write's own pointer root skipped when the forward reader calls it. AND A POINTER-VALUE RECEIVER OF A `Self->` METHOD (`hp.set()` where `set` writes `self.o`) writes through what `hp` points at, as `f(hp)` does (DEF-275): `acc_call` captures `recv_ptr_param` and routes a pointer receiver through `check_written` (→ the forward reader); a by-value receiver gets a copy and writes through nothing. `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the write, the call or the move. AND `decreases fuel` ON LANDING 159'S TWO RECURSIVE SLICE-TYPE HELPERS (`escape_type_may_hold_run`, `escape_view_carriers`), whose `stack-depth` rows stood `open` at landing 159: the recursion descends the type graph, `fuel` bounds it, and the two rows discharge. NOW, TWO RULES. THE STORAGE A SLICE VIEWS WHEN NO RANGE NAMES IT IS READ OFF ITS REFS (DEF-297, DEF-301; the plan's M2, `wt/44a/.internal/DEF297_301_PLAN.md`): `escape_slice_targets` (escape.npk) answers, for a slice value -- a slice local, a struct's slice field, a range of either, a call's result, a ternary's or a `pick`'s arms -- the refs `collect_view_refs` carries (a slice local's or a struct's chain through the identifier arm, a call's result by rule A) that may be the run it views, typed at the end of each ref's path (`escape_ref_is_run_of`, `escape_path_end_type`, `escape_type_may_hold_run`: an array, a slice or a `List` of the slice's element, or storage that may hold one; a run carried transitively counts too -- more targets, never fewer; a ref rooted nowhere, a `wild` slice's or an allocator's, gives none, D-315, D-361). Three readers. THE STORE: a slice argument no place names (`escape_is_unnamed_slice`: a call's result, a ternary, a `pick`) is a destination at last -- a FRAME view in the callee's store is `NITPICK-BORROW-009` at the argument with landing 157's sentence (`refuse_frame_view_into_slice`, one definition, two readers), and any other ref lands on each target as landing 156's walk records a store through a pointer or a slice local (`record_refs_into_unnamed_slice`; the walk's per-target body extracted as `record_refs_at_target` and `record_matrix_at`, one definition and two readers: a parameter-rooted target the matrix, a frame target its chain), so D-325's freeze refuses the pointee's later write (`NITPICK-BORROW-015`). THE SUMMARY: a callee's write through a slice local or field over its parameter's run (`t = sl; t[0i64].o = v`) is a write of that parameter's storage (`record_mut_through_slice`), and a slice argument no place names hands the callee's writes to each parameter-rooted target (`record_mut_of_slice_targets`), each target's WHOLE extent (`target_whole_path`). THE WRITE CHECK (alias.npk): a write through a slice no range of an array or a `List` names -- direct (`t[0i64].o = …`; the slice found walking inward from the written place, `escape_written_slice_base`), `@t[0i64]` handed to a writer, or the slice itself handed to one (`acc_call`, `check_written_slice`) -- is checked at EACH target, over the target's whole extent and never composed with the callee's path (a target may stand for a run at another level than the write's), against the view and lent parties rooted there (`check_slice_targets`, `check_view_parties_at`), every party the slice binding itself holds skipped BY ITS HOLDER (`escape_slice_own_holder`: a slice local, or a struct whose type carries exactly one view, its slice field -- `escape_view_carriers` -- whose chain entries can stand for nothing deeper): `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the write or the argument. And a range of a slice or of a slice parameter handed to a writer is composed with the callee's path at its trailing run's FIRST range (`escape_first_trailing_range`, in `record_mut_of_call` and `check_written_slice`), where 157 composed it at the argument and every later step stood one position too far (n297k, n297l). A RANGE'S ELEMENT IS ITS STORAGE'S ELEMENT (DEF-303): a path's text is LEVELLED (`level_path_text`, escape.npk, at `path_join` and `path_rel_compute`): `r<a>:<b>/i<n>` is `i<a+n>` and `r<a>:<b>/r<c>:<d>` the range `r<a+c>:<a+d>` when every bound is known and in [0, 2^31); otherwise the element is `e<lo>:<hi>`, somewhere in the range's extent, or `E`, in an extent nobody knows, and a range of a range nobody can add is `R`; a `pick` binding's opaque step is `P`, a level of its own, where it was `R` (levelled as `R`, it would absorb the step after it); the aliasing walk reads the three tokens (`decode_step`: `Step` kinds 3 and 4, compared as a range, rendered `[…]`) and levels both lists before every `overlap_class` (`level_steps`, the same rule), so the six are refused (`NITPICK-BORROW-015`; z303c `NITPICK-BORROW-002` at the store, the holder-scope question) and z303i is ADMITTED, as its direct spelling `arr[1i64].o = …` always was -- ONE REFUSAL REMOVED. THREE SAFE SHAPES COST, refused by the whole-extent check, the plan's conservative choice (each 0/0 at both legs under landing 158's compiler, none in the three trees): a writer handed a COPY of a slice local while the original lives (c297p, `H2[]:t2 = t; drop w(t2);`: the original is a view party -- hand the slice itself with no copy live), a view of ANOTHER element of the run (c297s: a view of `arr[1i64].o`, `drop w(t)` writing `sl[0i64].o` -- hand the range of the elements written, `drop w(arr[0i64...1i64])`, which stands; the whole range `arr[0i64...2i64]` is refused by 157's widening, fr3) and a view of ANOTHER field (c297t: a view of `arr[0i64].b`, a callee writing `sl[0i64].a` through `t` -- hand the range itself, `drop w5(arr[0i64...2i64])`, where the callee's path composes field by field, which stands); each spelling that stands checked under this compiler. The precise reading (a target's element and field composed with the callee's path when the target is the run itself and not a transitive approximation of a deeper one) is THE REFINEMENT NOT BUILT. Admitted, each 0/0 at both legs on both compilers: a slice local, a struct's slice field and a call's result handed to a writer with no view live (c297q, c297r, c301j), a direct write through a slice local and through a one-carrier struct's slice field with no view live (c297y, c297u, c297v), a COPY stored through a call's result (c301k). THE CHAIN'S FIRST BUILD WAS WRONG, AND THE SWEEP FOUND IT: on the landing's first tree the sweep reported five new BORROW-015 sites in nitpick-time's core (vec.npk 336:9, 427:6, 461:22; bytes.npk 166:9, 180:13; each repeated in every file importing them) -- `T[]:s = #wild_slice<T>(v.items, v.count); s[i] = x;` in a generic body, `move(s[…])`, and `room[b.len] = x` over a buffer's `ptr`, the conflicting party the slice's OWN view. `check_view_parties_at` skipped the slice's own party only when the party's place and path equalled the target's, and the slice's chain carries the parameter `v`'s value too (a parameter's value, typed as one that may hold the run), whose target is the whole of `v`, so the write met `s`'s own view of `v.items`; a non-generic `int32` element does not reproduce it (a pointer to a scalar holds no run). The skip is by holder alone now, as M2's design says (a slice's parties are its views of the runs it spans, and writing an element through it frees none of them); the probe that holds a slice viewing its own run's element bytes through a callee's store (o01, 113/113 on 158's compiler) stays refused at the same site, and `slice_targets_ok.npk`'s checks 21 and 22 hold the library's shapes. THE CAUSE (escape.npk): `escape_assign` records a stored value's refs on a root's chain only for a target `field_store_root` names -- a local or a by-value parameter reached through no pointer, `<-`, `?.` or slice step (landing 154) -- or for a bare local; a target through a pointer went to `record_flow_of_store`, which folded the value's refs into the function SUMMARY's store matrix for a pointer parameter's pointee and, for a pointer local, "wherever the local may point" -- but read only the local's refs rooted at a pointer PARAMETER (`param_index_of(x, pr.root) < 0` was a `continue`), so a pointer, a pointer field or a slice whose target was THIS frame's storage put the stored view on no chain, D-325's freeze had no party for it, and the pointee's write was admitted; and at a call the caller applied the callee's store matrix to the ARGUMENT's root -- the pointer local itself -- never to where the local points. NOW: A STORE THROUGH AN INDIRECTION LANDS WHERE THE INDIRECTION'S REFS SAY (`record_refs_through_indirection`, escape.npk: ONE walk, called by `record_flow_of_store` with the ORIGINAL target -- so `<-hp = v` is a store THROUGH `hp` -- and by `record_dest_refs` after the callee's store matrix is applied, where a bare pointer NAME handed as a destination is a store through it: the call road). The indirection is the root when it is a pointer or a slice, else the last pointer-typed base on the path (`path_pointer_base_type`: `o.inner.v` answers `Holder->`); its TARGETS are read off the root's chain BY TYPE -- an address whose place is the pointee's type (`hp = @h`), a parameter's value carried whose place is the pointer's own type (`hp = p2`), and for a slice a view of an array or a slice whose element is the slice's (the array it views, never what that array holds, which a view of it carries transitively). A target rooted at a POINTER PARAMETER is a store into that parameter's pointee -- the store matrix, as before; a target rooted at a LOCAL, a BY-VALUE parameter or a `for` binding is THIS frame's storage, and the stored refs are recorded on that root's chain (`frame_owned_key`, `escape_add_view_ref`'s key) -- what a direct field store records since landing 154 -- so D-325's freeze holds a party for the view and the pointee's later write is `NITPICK-BORROW-015` ("held by `h`"), in all eight faces and the slice's. A ref rooted nowhere (a `wild` pointer, an allocator's result) records nothing (D-315, D-361), and a pointer with no refs records nothing, as before. ADDITIVE: every record the old loop made is still made (a direct store through a pointer-typed root feeds the matrix from every parameter-rooted ref of its chain, unfiltered). NOW: a body nested in a body is walked ONCE per level by every front-half pass. (DEF-281) `assign_loop_body` walks a body once when the walk is already an enclosing loop's silent pre-pass (`x.quiet`): what a silent walk hands up is the four unions `state_absorb_may` takes (`may`, `moved`, `freed`, `sealed_any`), each the start state's bits plus what the body sets, and a second silent walk of the same body from a wider start adds nothing to them; the real pass still walks every loop it meets twice -- its own silent pre-pass, then the reporting walk -- so no report moves, and a nest of n loops is quadratic in n. (DEF-278) The escape walk's `give` arm ORs its operand's verdict into the open pick's frame (`Escape.givex`, pushed and popped around the arms' ONE walk in `escape_expr`'s pick arm), and `gives_borrowy`/`stmt_gives_borrowy` are gone; the statement walk's `give` arm types its operand ONCE against the pick's running type (`type_give_operand`, type_expr.npk; the frames `ExprTyper.givex_pick`/`givex_cur`, pushed by `type_pick_expr(e, id, n)` with the slot's expectation and read back after its arms), and `arm_give_type` is gone -- the rule it kept (the first give's type, every later give fitting it exactly, D-092's no-widening) is the frame's. (DEF-283) So a `give` anywhere in the arm -- at its top level, under an `if`, inside a loop -- is held to the pick's type: `NITPICK-TYPE-007` at the give, where it was typed against nothing. (DEF-284) And every arm of a `pick` used as a value ends in `give` or leaves: the bindings analysis asks `stmt_completes` of each arm in `assign_expr`'s pick arm -- a `give` never completes (the row added to the one judge; a give stands only in such an arm, TYPE-094, so FLOW-001's question of a function body is untouched), `fall` continues into the next arm, `pass`/`fail`/`exit`/a trap/`break`/`continue` leave, an `if` without `else`, a counted loop and a `when` complete as wholes, a `while (true)` with no `break` of its own never does -- and an arm that may complete is `NITPICK-FLOW-001` at the arm with its own sentence; D-323's conservatism stands (a give inside a counted loop or a `when` does not count: give after the loop, or restructure). NOW: `fold_call` resolves a comptime call's type arguments at the call -- in the caller's scope and under the caller's generic scope, before the frame is entered -- and binds the callee's generic window to them around the body's evaluation (a `GenericScope` of the window's parameters over the resolved ids, `bind_instance`'s shape; the ids committed to the AST's id pool per evaluation, as a generic CALL's are per site, the fuel bounding how many), with the ENCLOSING scope none: a `comptime func:` is a module-level declaration, and the call site's impl or function parameters are not its to see (DEF-192's rule). So `#size_of<T>()`, `T:y` and `T[3]` in the body mean the argument's type; a call with no turbofish binds nothing and folds as it always did (`same(21i32)`); a count that is not the window's is the checker's arity report, and the evaluator adds no second sentence (D-240); an argument the resolver refused has had its own sentence, and the call does not fold. A type argument that is the CALLER's own parameter (`g<U>` calling `width::<U>()`) resolves to that parameter, and the emitter's per-instance fold answers the number, as `comptime(#size_of<U>())` always did. And `move(x)` folds as its operand's value (`fold_k_move_expr`, the kind's arm in `fold_expr_rest`): the evaluator's values are copies, and the move rules stay the checker's, enforced on the body as on any (MOVE-001 on a later read). Before it: **DEF-265 AND DEF-271 LANDED 2026-10-21 — AFTER A FAILED EXPRESSION, A STRUCT LITERAL'S BRACES ARE THE EXPRESSION'S, AND A BLOCK WITHOUT ITS `{` READS NOTHING: THE STATEMENT LEVEL KNOWS A BRACE GROUP** (landing 139, `1.6.1e.md`'s record; built by `nitpick-compiler_40`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_41`; DEF-265, registered 2026-10-17 at landing 130 from the fuzz corpus's `tr0550`; planned in its OPEN_DECISIONS row; DEF-271 registered and fixed here, found by this landing's sweep) — AFTER A FAILED EXPRESSION, A STRUCT LITERAL'S BRACES ENDED THE ENCLOSING BODY, AND THE STATEMENTS AFTER IT WERE REPORTED AS DECLARATIONS (DEF-265, registered 2026-10-17 by `nitpick-compiler_40` at landing 130 from that landing's measurement of the fuzz corpus's `tr0550`; low: a count defect, DEF-245's family, every file refused either way): `Box<int32>:c = Box<int32>{ v: 5i32, n: 3i32 }; exit 0i32;` inside `main` (a generic struct literal is spelled bare, `Box{ … }`, so `Box < int32` reads as a comparison) was PARSE-002 "expected an expression" at `int32` and then PARSE-001 "expected a type" at `exit` -- `main`'s body had ended at the literal's `}` and `exit` stood at the module level. READ LINE BY LINE FIRST (the row's reading -- the statement recovery stopping at the `{` -- was half right): the expression failed at `int32` without consuming it; `p_recover` stopped there at once (a builtin type can start an item); the next statement failed at the same token (its identical report deduplicated); and the parser's no-progress belt, `p_sync`, stepped over `>` and the literal's `{` and stopped at its `}`, which the block loop took as the body's end. A `{` the statement recovery itself MET was the second face: `Foo<bar>{ v: 1i32 }` refused at the `{` (two comparisons) stopped `p_recover` there, and the literal's fields were parsed as a block's statements and reported again. MEASURED FIRST on a WIP compiler of the queue and again on landing 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def265/`): `stmt_brace.npk` 2 reports, `lit_brace.npk` 3, the fuzz corpus's `tr0550` 3, `as0594` 3, `ty1282` 2. AND A BLOCK WITHOUT ITS `{` READ WHAT FOLLOWED AS ITS STATEMENTS AND TOOK THE ENCLOSING BODY'S `}` AS ITS OWN (DEF-271, registered and fixed here, found by this landing's own sweep -- its first run had five fuzz programs gain a false report; DEF-245's family at the statement level): after a statement header that failed -- `for (int32:i = 0i32; i < 3i32; i += 1i32)` refused at `=` (D-023), `if (x = 3i32)`, `while (x = 3i32)` and `pick (x = 3i32)` at `=`, `loop(0i32, 3i32 1i32)` at the missing comma -- the parser stood short of the body's `{`, and `p_parse_block` ran its statement loop anyway: the rest of the header and the body were read as statements and the ENCLOSING block's `}` closed the headless block, so the enclosing `{` was "never closed" (PARSE-003 at it), or a later declaration's `}` closed it and that declaration was read as statements ("expected a type", "expected an expression") -- one mistake, three or four reports. DEF-265's brace group made it show where it had hidden: the belt kept the later declaration's `}` its own group's, and five fuzz programs whose `main` that `}` had closed (`ct0003`, `ct0170`, `ct0314`, `op0064`, `ty0982`) gained a PARSE-003 at `main`'s brace. MEASURED on landing 137's compiler and again on 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def271/`): each header shape inside `main` followed by a function four reports, the C `for` five; a lone `main` with the C `for` PARSE-003 at `main`'s `{`. AND THE CHAIN'S OWN FIRST READING: the headless rule alone left two phantoms of its neighbours -- a broken ARM's rest read as the enclosing block's statements (`ct0080` 4 -> 6 reports, `mc0374` 5 -> 8) and a failed `for` header's rest read as a declaration (`ty0982` 4 -> 2, one of them "expected `;`, found `in`") -- read site by site before the texts, each closed below. NOW: the statement level knows a brace group, as the declaration level has since landing 130. `p_sync`, the belt every parser loop calls when a rule consumed nothing, counts `{` and `}` and stops at a sync point only at depth 0 -- a `}` it meets after skipping a `{` is the group's, never the enclosing block's end; and `p_recover` skips a `{` that follows a name or a `>` whole (`p_skip_brace_group`), as the failed expression's literal, and goes on to the statement's end. A `{` after `;`, `}`, `{`, `)` or a keyword (`else`, `then`, `defer`) is still a block: a block statement after a failed statement's `;`, and an `if`'s body after a failed condition, are parsed as they were. And a block without its `{` reads nothing (DEF-271): `p_parse_block`, entered short of its `{` after a statement header failed, is an empty block, and what follows is the enclosing block's, read under its recovery -- a `{` there is the body, parsed as a block statement -- so the enclosing `}` stays the enclosing block's. AND TWO RULES THE CHAIN'S READING ADDED (its first run over 5,241 files put six new sites in three fuzz programs, each a phantom of the headless rule's neighbours): A FAILED PARENTHESISED HEADER IS SKIPPED TO ITS `)` (`p_recover_header`, parser.npk: `if`, `while`, `when`, the `for` and a `pick`'s selector -- parentheses and brace groups counted, a `{` met first left alone as the body's, a `}` that would close the enclosing block left standing, panic cleared so the body that follows is the statement's own), because the header's rest -- the C `for`'s clauses after `=`, `int64:i in 0i64...3i64` after a refused qualifier -- begins with what a declaration begins with and the enclosing block's recovery read it as statements; and AN ARM'S RECOVERY IS TO THE NEXT ARM (`p_recover_arm`, parse_pick.npk: the `,` or the pick's `}` at bracket depth 0; and the arms without their `{` read nothing, as a headless body does), because a pattern that is no pattern (`MyMacro!(a, b) where (a > b) { … }`, a removed form) had its `(a, b)` read as the enclosing block's statements once the headless block stopped eating it -- two phantoms where the statement recovery stops at the name. So a failed `if`, `while`, `when`, `for` (the C form too) or `pick` header, a body whose `{` is missing and a broken arm are ONE report each. NOW: a value pattern is a constant of the selector's type (`check_value_patterns`, type_stmt.npk, called from `type_pick_rules`, both spellings). Each value and each range bound is typed under the selector's type -- an unsuffixed literal takes its width and must fit it (TYPE-031, the literal's own rule) -- and held to that type exactly (TYPE-007: no conversion enters a case); it is a constant the compile-time folder decides -- a literal, a negated literal, a `fixed` module binding, `comptime(…)`, constant arithmetic -- or TYPE-007 naming the guard spelling `(*) where (v == n) { … }` for a value known at run time (a local string pattern over a string, which ran as a run-time comparison, is refused with it); a range needs an ordered selector (an integer, a `char`, a kernel identifier, a twisted, ternary or fixed-point value: TYPE-007); and a float selector is refused whole, at the selector (TYPE-065, the code of a selector no case can be taken over, with an `Optional`'s and a pointer's): `==` on a float is no decision. NOW, two rules in one walk. (a) THE HEADER BAIL: every declaration parser with a body, a member list or an initialiser tests `p_in_panic` after its header and before the part that recovers on its own -- `p_parse_func` (the contracts and the body), `p_parse_record` (the `{`, the member loop and the close), `p_parse_trait` and `p_parse_impl` (the item block), `p_parse_rules`, `p_parse_macro` (the body scan, the `{`, the loop and the close), `p_parse_extern` (the member loop), `p_parse_extern_fn` (the failure clause), `p_parse_global`, `p_parse_errdecl` and `p_parse_unitdecl` (the initialiser) -- and returns its own kind with empty windows: the header's report is the one report, a body read in panic (whose statement recovery would leave panic and report the misaligned rest as new mistakes) is never read, and the walks that run before the driver stops (the depth pass, the file-header check) read a well-formed node. `p_parse_mod` is the one exception and says why: a member-less module node is a FILE import to the loader, so `mod:error = { … }` bailed was "cannot find module ``" -- a second sentence, found by the kinds probe; its block is read as before, and a sound block read in panic reports nothing. (b) THE RECOVERY KNOWS A BRACKET AND A TERMINATOR: `p_recover_decl` stops at `is_decl_sync_point`'s set -- Eof, `;`, `}`, `pub`, the declaration keywords (`mod`, `rules`, `assoc`, `opaque`, `error` and `unit` among them) and the modifiers `async`/`thread`/`inline`/`noinline`; not the statement keywords, which `is_sync_point` keeps for `p_sync` and a statement's recovery; not `comptime`, which opens `comptime(…)` inside a type; not `#`, which opens `#size_of<T>()` inside one -- at depth 0 only, where `{`, `(` and `[` raise the depth and their closers lower it: a `;` at depth 0 is consumed, a `}` at depth 0 is left standing (the enclosing item block's end), a `)` or `]` at depth 0 is the broken header's own and stepped over; and a recovery that begins right after a consumed `;` skips nothing (DEF-260: the broken declaration ended at its own terminator, and what follows is the next member -- `p_prev_kind`). A macro's declaration body recovers per member as an item block does (without it a member whose header failed left the loop in panic and the member's own `}` ended the macro). And `p_parse_module` eats a `}` or a `;` left standing at the top level right after a declaration that reported -- a body whose `{` was the mistake (`func:f = int32() pass 1i32; };` is refused at `pass`, its recovery ends at the `;`, and the `}` was "expected a type") -- never after a sound declaration and never inside an item block. The parser's success path is untouched: a sound program's tree is the same tree, and nothing past the parser moves. THE RULES, one walk: (R1, D-339 (a)) THE FLOOR IS CLOSED -- a call that resolves to a TRAIT's method bounded `acquires <= N` (through a `dyn`, a bound `T: Trait`, `Trait.m(x)`, or a concrete receiver whose impl runs the default) is legal only with NOTHING held, `NITPICK-LOCK-001` otherwise with the floor's own sentence ("a `<=` bound is a ceiling only, so an implementation may take any level at or below it ... declare the trait method's level exactly (`acquires N`), or take the guard after the call"), and the caller is FLOORLESS -- propagated by the widening like a level (`floorless`, `floor_via`), so a call of IT while anything is held is refused the same way, naming the bounded method it reaches through; (R2, hole 1) a call that resolves to an IMPLEMENTATION's method inheriting `<= N` is judged by the body's own summary, the ceiling checked where the body is walked as before; (R3, D-339 (a)) EXACT IS EXACT -- every call of a method under an exact `acquires N` on the trait, or of an implementation of it, is judged at N (`call_lo`/`call_hi`), and an implementation (or default body) whose summary reaches BELOW N, or reaches through a `<=`-bounded method, is `NITPICK-LOCK-002` where it is written as one reaching above N was, so the contract IS the body's summary once the body is legal and a refused body is reported once (D-240: `lock_undeclared.npk`'s case 6b moves from LOCK-001 at the direct call to LOCK-002 at `Shallow.open`); Before it: **D-359 LANDED 2026-10-17 — THE ORPHAN RULE: AN IMPL NAMES AT LEAST ONE TYPE OR TRAIT DECLARED IN ITS OWN MANIFEST'S TREE, `NITPICK-TYPE-099` AT THE IMPL** (landing 124, `1.6.1e.md`'s record; `nitpick-compiler_39`; the user's ruling of 2026-10-15 at 07:54:31 through `management_0`, S-37's recommendation: "go with the S-37 recommendation"; planned first at `.internal/handoff_35/D359_PLAN.md`) — a program could implement a prelude trait for a builtin or a prelude type the prelude did not cover -- `impl:bool:Ord` was measured admitted at 1.5.2b (S-37), while `impl:int32:Ord` met the prelude's row under coherence (TYPE-013) -- and two libraries doing it would collide in the program that imports both, with no impl of the program's own at fault and no sentence saying which library to change; **`NITPICK-TYPE-099` (`TYPE_ORPHAN_IMPL`, type_codes.npk) at the impl, once, asked in `collect_one_impl` (type_trait.npk) after the target and the trait resolve (a refused one has had its sentence, D-240). A file's TREE is the directory of the nearest `nitpick.toml` above it (`srcmgr_tree_of`, source.npk: the walk `front_set_root` makes for the main file, D-236, made once per file and kept in `SourceFile.tree`), else the program's root -- a manifest-less project is one tree -- and "<synthetic>" for a file nothing opened, so the prelude is its own tree and a derive's generated impl, which names its subject by construction, is never asked. The impl names a type of its tree when the target's declaration, or ANY named type among its type arguments or element types (`type_names_tree`, the shape of `type_mentions_params_of`), is declared in a file of that tree -- `impl:List<Pt>:ToString` names `Pt`; a generic parameter and a builtin name nothing -- and a trait of its tree when the trait segment's declaration is; an inherent impl's target must be the tree's own, a blanket impl's trait must be. The refused entry STAYS in the impl table, marked `orphan`, so a call through it finds its method and reports nothing more (1.6.1c's lesson), and `check_coherence` and `check_impls_complete` skip it: `impl:int32:Ord` is TYPE-099 where it was TYPE-013 against the prelude's row, `impl:string:Copy` TYPE-099 where it was TYPE-087. The **plan is in `meta/roadmap/`**,
organised as numbered cycle folders holding `x.y.z.md` subcycle files; finished
cycles move to `meta/roadmap/done/`. Start at `meta/roadmap/ROADMAP.md`.

**Cycles 0.0–1.0 are done** (`meta/roadmap/done/`): the lexer, the AST and parser,
the module/symbol/visibility passes, the type system, the static analyses, macros
with `comptime` and `#[derive]`, IR emission, `nlibc` and the runtime floor, full
type lowering, the memory allocator, and generics/traits/`dyn`. **`npkc` exists**:
`src/npkc.npk` over `src/driver/pipeline.npk` (the one front-half sequence;
`tools/check.npk` is a thin wrapper over it) and `src/backend/`. The harness runs
**272 real-backend programs** (the count at the 1.5.6b close — this sentence said 172 from cycle 1.0 until then; each also re-run through `opt -O2` + `llc -O2`
since 1.3.8) and, since 1.5.4 retired the rung suite, asserts NO `NITPICK-RUNG-001` rejection (1 until 1.5.4 lowered `prove`/`assert_static`; 4 until 1.5.3 lowered the three contract cases; 6 until 1.5.2 lowered the two `limit<Rules>` cases; 8 until 1.4.7's
OWED-8 moved the two channel-element cases to the type checker as `TYPE-057`), and **stage 1 rebuilds itself byte-identically** — the fixpoint has held
through every cycle since 0.8.

**Cycle 1.1 (async and concurrency) is underway.** Landed: `never fails` checked
everywhere with `raw`/`drop` licensed by it (D-163), the `Duration` clock, coroutine
lowering as hand-written switched-resume state machines (D-177/D-178), the typed
`Error` system with origin chains and an exhaustive `failsafe` (D-179), per-thread
executors, real threads over `clone(2)` (D-181), and `atomic<T>` plus channels,
thread pools and actors (D-182). **Cycle 1.2 — the managed
lowering — was inserted ahead of the rest** (D-183): 1.1.11's `Mutex` hands out a
guard whose release IS scope exit, and until this cycle the default regime
implemented no drops at all. Landed through 1.2.3e: the cap==0 ownership bit,
per-type generated `@"npk.drop.<tid>"` bodies, drop flags with move/`pass`
clearing, the move-only rule for owning types (TYPE-046/047), `move T:p`
consuming parameters, and scope-exit drops LIVE for strings, structs and enums
— the compiler drops its own locals and still rebuilds itself byte-identically.
Debug instruments that stay: 0xAA free-poisoning, and `@npk_quarantine`
(npkrt.ll, ships 0) — a never-reuse mode that makes any use-after-free
deterministic, with a poisoned-source tripwire in `npk_string_concat`, and **`NPK_HEAP_STATS`** (1.5.1b step 0): a program whose environment carries
that name prints `heap: allocated=<n> peak_live=<n> count=<n>` — the
allocator's own words, deterministic for a given input — on fd 2 at exit; the
harness's `cost` stage and `npkg test` read it, and a wall-clock is never a
verdict.
**Cycle 1.2 is COMPLETE** (`meta/roadmap/done/1.2/`): drops are live for
strings, structs, enums, `dyn` (which owns a heap cell and drops through
the vtable's slot 0) and arenas, in sync AND async bodies (frame-resident
flags; the wind-up unwind drops too, and the first test to drive a wind-up
found the rouse and the grace-wait defects). Channels reclaim at their
creating function's exit — `StaleHandle` is reachable, slots reuse under
moved generations — and OWNING elements cross channels under the heap's
first futex mutex, with `move` required at the send. A factory says
`gives` after its parameter list (1.2.6): the caller's exit then owns the
returned channels' reclaim, and creating a channel in an unmarked
channel-returning function refuses. A failed `send` drops its element —
the rule a failing callee already applies to its `move` parameters. Owning elements in ARENAS
refuse at `arena_make` until the generated deep-view family lands (D-183).
Phase C renumbered around the cycle (self-hosting 1.3, verification 1.4,
Astrée 1.5; the map is in `ROADMAP.md`). **1.1.11 is COMPLETE**: `Mutex<T,LEVEL>`/`Guard<T>` (the guard's scope-exit
drop IS the release), `RwLock<T,LEVEL>` (`read` → read-only `RGuard<T>`,
`write` → the same `Guard<T>`), `CondVar<LEVEL>` (`timedwait` lends the
guard and reacquires; a timed-out wait SPENDS it — nulled, not held past
the deadline), and `Barrier<N,LEVEL>` (a timed-out party hands its slot
back). Levels feed the 0.5.6 ordering analysis from the receiver's types;
borrows of the primitives are the sanctioned spawn crossings. **1.1.12 (the
reactor) is underway — a landed (D-184, B-3a closed)**: epoll WITHOUT
timerfd (`epoll_pwait` as the armed executor's idle wait, carrying the
sleeper deadline), an eventfd as the cross-thread wake channel,
`suspend_io` + prelude `io_ready` + deferred `io_unwatch` (a registration
lives exactly as long as its wait), and **the task-identity rule**: awaits
drive children inline, so EVERY waiter registration — channels and locks
included — resolves the executor's `cur_task`, not the frame it was
lowered in; the nested-wait lost-wakeup this fixed was latent everywhere
(`nested_wait.npk` regresses it). `sys` takes pointers (`ptrtoint` at the
trampoline — the one place an address becomes a number). **1.1.12b landed
(D-185)**: `OwnedFd` (TY 39 — the drop IS the close; `own_fd`/`release_fd`;
move-only; a channel refuses it, a spawn MOVE is join-bounded legal),
`Path` + `path_parse` (constructor FUNCTIONS — the language has no static
methods), the `Reader`/`Writer` traits (`Self->` receivers, `within`) and
`ByteReader`/`ByteWriter` as prelude retry loops over `io_ready`
(`WouldBlock`/`Interrupted` are named prelude errnos), `Whence` seek, and
TYPE-048 (an impl keeps its trait's `async` — a sync body driven as a
coroutine is corruption). The awaited-method child frame now seeds `Self->`
receivers by ADDRESS. S-1 was **settled by the user as D-186**:
`string_slice` returns an OWNED COPY (`string_from_bytes` stays the
explicit view primitive), and the fallout closed D-183's field/element
overwrite leak — assigning over an owning FIELD or managed-array ELEMENT
now drops the old value (`overwrite_owned.npk` proves both by descriptor
exhaustion). **1.1.12c
landed**: the text layer as GENERIC WRAPPERS — `TextWriter<W>`,
`LineBufWriter<W>` (buffering is a TYPE, never a mode field), `LineEnding`,
unconditional read translation with the split-`\r\n` pending flag,
`string_bytes`, and `std_in`/`std_out`/`std_err` constructors that each own
a CLOEXEC dup (std descriptors stay blocking — the one stated D-071
exception). Three compiler fixes fell out: template fields must resolve
through `struct_field`'s BOUND walk (three raw sites closed — the drop-body
generator was reporting "no type named `W`" on nested generic instances),
the `Ident { Ident :` struct-literal lookahead reads the fourth token, and
awaited bound-calls in generic bodies substitute before trusting the
recorded template symbol. **1.1.12d landed — 1.1.12 (the reactor and the
I/O surface) is COMPLETE**: async methods behind `dyn` — the method slot
holds the concrete RESUME, a size tail (`@"npk.fsz.<frame>"` globals) tells
the caller how big the frame is, and the await site builds it from the
TRAIT's shape through a per-site prefix type and drives the standard loop.
Object safety: async + `Self->` receiver is safe (by-value `Self` alone
still disqualifies); `Self->` receivers admitted generally — and the sync
thunks' by-value-receiver latent (handing a method its own first bytes as
an address) is fixed. `dyn_stream.npk`: one erased `report(dyn Writer…)`,
two writers, two byte patterns. **1.1.13 (the Bridge, D-149 over D-055) is
underway — stage a is COMPLETE (D-187, D-188)**: `#ptr_add<T>`
element-scaled; `atomic_from_ptr::<T>` fused-only over the turbofish;
`lib/nbridge.npk`'s sealed shm (`shm_create_sealed` — the seal is the
architecture's load-bearing line), SCM_RIGHTS pair, and `spawn_driver` →
`bridge_reap` over `npk_driver_clone_exec` (fork-shape clone, allocation-free
child, PDEATHSIG + NO_NEW_PRIVS + dup3 + execve); the DRIVER REGISTRY is
published BEFORE the clone (CLONE_PIDFD writes the pidfd into the slot),
`npk_driver_kill_all` runs on the TRAP PATH before user `failsafe`, and a
clean exit 0 with a live driver refuses — trap −4109 `DriverLeak` (D-188,
the D-151 exit rule's second registry). The Bridge tier never traps: `?!` is
unwrap-or-TRAP-as (a test-main assert, barred from the Bridge by v3 §4.2 —
an EPIPE schedule caught the misuse), so the library binds-and-fails. The
harness grew `tests/backend/fixtures/` + `// argv:` substitution
(`mock_driver.npk` speaks the wire), and the `// stress:` loop — dead in the
real-backend stage until now — runs there again. **Stage b is COMPLETE
(D-189)**: the §6.1 ring header (power-of-two capacity a spawn parameter),
`io_watch` (register-only, two args) + prelude `io_ready2`, dispatch —
descriptor before the SeqCst head publish, EXEC_NOTIFY, the TRIPLE wait
(ctrl/pidfd/stderr), [UNTRUSTED] tail and reply validation,
kill-on-protocol, KILL-ON-DEADLINE (a hang ends now; the graceful §5.4
ladder is close's alone), poisoning — `bridge_close` (rungs end on
DEATH-OR-BUDGET, never on a wait merely returning; the release phase
synchronous behind `closed`), and the stderr tail WOVEN into the waits
(D-180 bars a borrowing drain task) with `bridge_stderr_tail` the reader.
The work flushed two runtime defects: the executor never CONSUMED the
waker's due-now stamp at resume, so every once-woken task busy-polled all
its later waits (a year latent behind retry loops; one cmpxchg at
`npk_step`'s resume site), and D-186's slice copy allocated from the
wild-TRACKED entry (a sliced string alive at `exit 0` was a phantom
WildLeak). **Stage c is COMPLETE (D-190) — 1.1.13 and the Bridge are
DONE**: `extern` blocks lower to GENERATED stubs (`bridge_stubs.npk`, the
derive mechanism's sibling — source synthesized and spliced before
collection; the block binds nothing, the stubs are ordinary async fns,
zero downstream special cases), methods declared IN FULL (`Bridge->`
first, `Duration` last, the v1 wire vocabulary between; departures refuse
EXTERN-001, written contracts EXTERN-002 — D-002's mandatory-contract
parse rule lifted as D-149 scheduled), the INTERFACE HASH rides INIT_REQ
under D-179's error-identity seed (one derived-identity constant
ecosystem-wide) and a stale driver refuses at the handshake, and
`sdk/npkdrv.h` + harness-built C reference drivers prove the wire end to
end (`extern_c_driver.npk`: echo through the ring, driver-reported
refusal → EDriverError, hostile tail → protocol kill, stale interface →
EDriverSpawn). **CYCLE 1.1 IS COMPLETE** (D-191, `done/1.1/`): user
variadics and `..^` landed at the close (the spec's "a variadic call lowers
to building one" — gather, spread, empty tail, and the awaited gather in a
frame slot; methods may not collect, by refusal), the await-edge rungs
became checker rules (SUSPEND-003: no await in a `where` guard) and
internal belts, and the close-out's first exercised programs found three
latent defects: the collector's name was never typable in a body, a
range-view was not counted as address-taking, and stage D's "lives to the
function's end" extension had been a silent no-op since birth (`fn_end`
read off a block span that covers only the opening — every address-taken
local whose last textual use preceded a later suspension sat on the dying
resume stack). **G-3 settled (the user): the exotic tier is NEW CYCLE 1.3**,
and Phase C renumbered a second time — self-hosting 1.4, verification 1.5,
and 1.6 (which was Astrée until **D-233** re-homed the evidence to the emitted
IR; the cycle numbers are unchanged, its topic is not). Adopting `dyn Writer` in npkc's own diagnostics is 1.4
material. **The 1.1 interlude closed the self-contained backlog**: `sys` is
TYPED (D-192 — `Result<int64>` by D-048's contract, register-shaped
arguments refused by name, zext for unsigned/kernel args, `?|` given `?!`'s
unknown-operand fallback; the S-3 rows), and float/char `ToString` landed
(D-193, §6b — shortest-round-trip Dragon4 over `uint2048` in the PRELUDE,
no wide division by construction, 353 python-generated known-answer
vectors; total UTF-8 with U+FFFD for non-scalars). The bare-builtin
signature table is proposed as P-3 (OPEN_DECISIONS §3), owed before 1.4.
**Cycle 1.3 (the exotic numeric tier) is COMPLETE** (`meta/roadmap/done/1.3/`) — 1.3.0 ratified the
whole surface as D-194…D-200 (the survey corrected the tier's story: `tfp`
is Twisted FIXED Point per D-036/D-037, the prototype's floating tfp_ops is
the superseded design; `dim256` units are exponent vectors with `unit:`
declarations ratified; ternary Kleene logic rides `&`/`|`; frac is
invariant-normalized exact-or-ERR; tensors cap at rank 9 with inline dims).
**1.3.1 landed `simd<T, N>`**: TY_SIMD through every walker (the B-7 sweep
enumerated up front), annotation-directed `simd(…)` with splat, elementwise
ops/compares (verdicts are `simd<bool, N>`), bounds-checked lane places,
`.len`, ordered extract-chain reductions (no intrinsics — nothing can
become a libcall), elementwise casts, and D-007 any-lane division guards
(zero → DivByZero, structural INT_MIN/−1 → DivOverflow). TYPE-029 (the
Tier-1 vector refusal) retired with the ctor rung; `.any` joined `.acquire`
in the keyword-after-dot interning. `simd_basic`/`simd_div0`/`simd_divmin`/
`simd_oob` + `simd_rules.npk` (14 refusals). **1.3.2 landed `tfp*`** —
twisted fixed point on native `i32..i256`, the tbb machinery generalized
to both twisted kinds (compare-trap, add/sub, `%`, negation, ERR, is_err)
plus Q-scaled mul/div through widened intermediates with round-trip
narrowing; literals fold EXACTLY in subset-1 limb arithmetic (the seed
still builds src/ — C-13); the cast matrix with sentinel maps and
trap-on-ERR-exit under both spellings; `.floor()`/`.trunc()`; exact-decimal
`ToString` (uint256 core, 100 python-`fractions` vectors); REACH refined
(twisted division arms no DivByZero; TbbErr armed for both families).
Three D-195 amendments recorded: compare-on-ERR TRAPS (D-008 §5 outranked
the carried spec row), raw bitwise/shifts STRUCK (`ERR << 1` is an ERR
laundry), tbb↔tfp casts impossible. **The flagged tbb asymmetry was then
settled by the user and landed as the D-144 amendment**: leaving tbb, ERR
traps under BOTH spellings (`=>!` acknowledges the VALUE's loss; the
0.9.5 carrier read crossed taint as INT_MIN), and the value crossing is
range-classified like any numeric pair — closing a second hole where
`tbb64 => int8` truncated and `tbb32 => uint32` sign-extended raw under
the CHECKED spelling; the prelude's four `tbbN:Hash` impls now spell
their guarded crossing `=>!`. **1.3.3 landed `dim256<Unit>`** — units as
packed 7-exponent SI vectors on interned `TY_DIM` (unit equality IS
type-id equality; a=256 keeps `type_int_bits` uniform), THE ZERO VECTOR
IS `tfp256` (cancellation yields the bare number, scaling needs no
special case, bare `dim256` refuses — one meaning, one spelling),
`unit:Name = algebra;` in the grammar (`unit` is now a keyword) with the
seven SI base names compiler-known and the derived set as PRELUDE
declarations, single-name annotations, `*`//`/` composing vectors with
`+ - %`/compare demanding equality (both units shown in base-product
form, TYPE-049/050), the one cast boundary (`=> tfp256` drops — ERR
RIDES, the crossing never leaves the family; `tfp256 =>! dim256<U>`
asserts), no `ToString` by design (`&{x => tfp256}` is the spelling),
and erased lowering (the TY_TFP-site sweep at 256 bits). Two found
defects: the `pick` ERR-arm demand had not extended to `tfp` (the
wildcard swallowed the taint — now all three twisted kinds demand it),
and **compound assignment was a second implementation of the operators**
— raw `add`/`sdiv` since 0.9.5, so a twisted `+=` wrapped, `/=` by zero
reached the hardware, and a float `+=` emitted integer `add`; the
arithmetic value core is now extracted (`emit_arith_value`) and BOTH
spellings route through it, with `op=` held to the slot's unit.
**1.3.4 landed the ternary/nonary bases** (`trit`/`tryte`/`nit`/`nyte`,
D-197): ONE `TY_TERN` kind, the binary rung storing the VALUE (balanced
order IS numeric order; the sentinels coincide with tbb's carrier
minimums, so `tbb_min_decimal`/`tbb_divlike` serve a third family; the
prototype's packed-trit LUTs are deliberately not carried — §7's
emulation-as-identity warning), overflow past the BALANCED bound → ERR
(the prototype's trit clamp OVERRULED by D-197's uniform text), `/ %` at
the multi-digit pair only (TYPE-051), the user-ratified Kleene `&`/`|`
on the single digits (min/max, NOT = `0 - x`), `.trit(i)`/`.nit(i)`
digit extraction by the offset trick (bounds-checked, ERR-sticky;
`trit`/`nit` joined the after-dot keyword interning), contextual
literals in any base checked EXACTLY against ±29524-style bounds, the
one-family cast matrix with the D-144-as-amended leaving rule, and four
prelude `ToString` impls. Found at implementation: **the same-text
early-out defeated the tbb cast matrix at SAME-WIDTH crossings** —
`tbb32⇄int32` share `i32`, so the crossings 0.9.5's audit hole was
ABOUT rode through unchecked, passing by coincidence (INT_MIN's bit
pattern IS the sentinel); the tbb intercept now precedes the early-out
as tfp's has since 1.3.2. **1.3.5 landed the `frac*` exact rationals**
(D-198): the tier's first NON-SCALAR family — `{whole: iN, num: iN,
denom: uN}`, five invariants after every operation, exact or ERR. The
algorithms are the PRELUDE's, in Nitpick, in `int256` (one core, the
width's bounds as parameters; the emitter unpacks → calls the
deterministic prelude symbol → repacks-or-ERR — the arithmetic a
verifier reads is source, not hand IR); operators `+ - * /` and
comparisons exactly (TYPE-052 gates `%`, pick selectors, and literals —
`int => frac` is the entry); read-only members `.whole/.num/.denom`;
canonical ERR `{minN, minN, 0}` with the disjunctive `is_err`; casts
widen `=>`/narrow `=>!`-absorbing, `=>! flt64` rounds, `=>! int`
truncates toward zero, ERR traps both spellings on exit, and a float
NEVER enters. Found: D-169's non-scalar compare belt called the first
checker-ADMITTED aggregate comparison a defect (the frac arm now
precedes it), plus two reserved-word collisions (`tid`, `fd`) in new
emitter code that only npkc's own parse catches. **1.3.6 landed
`complex<T>`** (D-199): `{T, T}` over the ratified four elements,
`complex(re, im)` reusing the ctor node (the payload names the
keyword), the arithmetic in per-element PRELUDE cores — tfp bodies ride
the language's own Q operators with the any-component-ERR → BOTH
canonicalization an explicit line, float bodies write SMITH'S division
(the naive denominator overflows at √max) with flt32 computing in
flt32 — equality only (no total order exists; per-component, NaN-aware,
taint-trapping), `is_err`/`ERR` on tfp elements only, NO casts either
direction, methods `.re/.im/.conj/.abs2` everywhere and `.abs` float-only
(`llvm.sqrt` declared in the fixed block), four concrete-instance
prelude `ToString` impls ("3+4i"; ERR pairs render "ERR"), TYPE-053.
**1.3.7 landed the library tier** (D-200): `buffer` minimally as the
MANAGED owning byte cell (`buffer_new(int64)` never fails — zeroed,
`len == cap == n`, `n <= 0` the empty answer; the string's trio, the
string's shared drop body, move-only, `==`-refused; §23's draft verb
family/`buffer_free`/`resize` struck by decision — typed access is
`#ptr_add` + `<-`; the checked byte place `b[i]` and byte view `b[lo...hi]`
since D-341, landing 128), `#sqrt` (flt32/64 → `llvm.sqrt.*`, the instruction —
a Newton loop computes a DIFFERENT number), `lib/nvec.npk` (vec2/3/4
over `simd<flt64, N>`, ctor FUNCTIONS, dot/cross/length2/length; vec9 as
nine named `flt64` fields with identity and 3×3 mul) and
`lib/ntensor.npk` (`matrix<T>` `{buffer, rows, cols}`, `tensor<T>` rank
≤ 9 dims INLINE, library bounds `fail`s — `tmatrix`/`ttensor` are
`matrix<tryte>` instances, the twisted ERR proven THROUGH the
container). Found: a checker-typed never-fails builtin's Result envelope
rode out under the bare recorded type — `buffer:b = buffer_new(16i64)`
stored 32 bytes into a 24-byte slot (the generic rt path now extracts at
the site; `buffer_new` is the first of its class); a generic struct
literal in a generic factory is the BARE `matrix{ … }` (the
`TextWriter{}` idiom — `matrix<T>{ … }` does not parse); checker-typed
builtins are called WITHOUT `raw` (`own_fd` precedent).
**1.3.8 closed the cycle**: the tier's lowering pinned in the ir_types
fixture (fifteen `ll_is` assertions, no `// ll:` markers — the seed
refuses every tier type, so the pin is the real compiler asserting its
own text), the last G-3 rung retired to a defect confession, and the
harness grew the **opt-O2 leg**: EVERY real-backend program re-runs
through `opt -O2` + `llc -O2` with the same expected exit (stress loops
included), the zero-dependency scan repeated on the optimised object
(`opt` may MINT libcalls), and a missing `opt` failing loudly. The
prototype's optimiser-removed-guarantee lesson is now a standing
instrument, not a one-off audit — and its FIRST full run caught a real
one: the suspend walk recorded no suspension point for the bare
primitives (`suspend_io`/`suspend_until`), so `io_ready`'s `give_up`
deadline sat in an ALLOCA that re-entry re-created — every run since
1.1.12 passed because the fresh slot happened to hold the OLD BYTES at
-O0, and the first optimised build moved the caller's frame and broke
eleven programs deterministically. The walk now records the park as a
suspension (the await rule: past the arguments), `give_up` lives in the
frame, and both levels answer identically. The second hole in the same
analysis (D-191's `fn_end` was the first).
**CYCLE 1.4 (self-hosting) is COMPLETE** (`meta/roadmap/done/1.4/`; closed 2026-09-02 at 1.4.9). 1.4.0 ratified the whole batch
as **D-201…D-209** (`meta/roadmap/done/1.4/1.4.0.md`): the builtin surface
typed from ONE generated signature table (D-201 — never-fails builtins

THE FLOOR'S PERMANENT FORM IS HAND-WRITTEN LLVM IR (D-203 — the user:
the goal was removing the C/C++ layer, never LLVM; npkrt.ll re-homes to

(overflow TRAPS on plain ints, `const`/`fixed`-only module state, the

channel-carrying concretes, the nfs safety riders, the schedule-

without asking. 1.4.1 landed the instruments (B-7's walkers-total check
found and fixed the missing TY_ENUM drop arm, a silent payload leak live
since 1.2, plus the contains-walker laundering holes). **1.4.2 (P-3) is
COMPLETE** — four commits, full harness green at each:
BUILTIN_REFERENCE.md's marked regions are now the ONE signature
authority, parsed strictly (`gen_tables.py` hard-fails on a row it cannot
read) and cross-checking the Signature column's `Result<…>` against the
Fails column; `builtins.npk` gained `builtin_sig_special/_count/_param/
_param_move/_ret` and **`ir_runtime.npk` is GENERATED from the same
rows** — the LLVM ABI derived, with a five-token `**ABI:**` note
(`inline`, `sym=`, `ret=`, `args=`, `envelope`) only where a symbol

regular builtin call is TYPED (arity, per-argument fit, `move`, spread
refusal; a `never fails` builtin is the BARE value, a may-fail one a
`Result<T>` — D-201 §4), four bespoke arms retired into the table,
`NITPICK-TYPE-054` carries a builtin's own call rules, and **2,215
`raw`/`relay` plus 242 `drop` came off the tree** in 150 files, with the
seed's three wrapped-flag flips and its extract-at-site in the same
commit. The emitter's parallel authority is gone: `result_ll_value_half`
and its `?|`/`?!` consumers, `emit_raw`'s wrapped/inner fallback,
`drop`'s nine hardcoded builtin names, and the argument coercion's blind
`zext` (the D-192 class — a negative `int32` widened into an enormous
positive `int64`) which now reads signedness off the recorded type.
Found on the way: **five latent unchecked pointer reinterpretations in
`src/`** (`ralloc`/`alloc` bytes bound or cast without `=>!`, invisible
while the callee had no type) and the instrument defect that
`check_runtime_sigs_agree`'s derived-inner cross-check had NEVER RUN — it
read the wrapped flag out of a leaked loop variable. Three of step 3's
four defects were caught by the compiler checking ITSELF, none by a test.
**1.4.2b** (D-210 overflow traps on plain ints, D-211 `fixed`-only module
state), **1.4.2c** (D-222 — `const` retired), **1.4.3** (D-208: the move
analysis learns about PARAMETERS, which is where the hole actually was — 26
findings in `src/`, including a live double free), and **1.4.3b** (D-216: the
consuming `pick (move(v))`) followed. **1.4.4 (D-207) is COMPLETE**:
`join_head` is per SCOPE — by a MARK, since the list is already a LIFO stack,
so a scope's exit joins until the head is back where its entry left it (one
pointer per scope, frame-resident at role 41 in a coroutine because a scope
spans suspensions). The order at every scope exit is now **join → defers →
drops → that scope's channel reclaims**, innermost first; D-183 ran the joins
LAST, which put a spawned child's borrowed `Mutex` behind the mutex's own
drop, and `type_drops`' "by the join discipline nobody can still hold it"
comment was a claim the lowering did not keep. `%npk.join` — one block every
exit branched to, and the reason the join ran last — is gone: the return seam
stores the result BEFORE the unwind and returns after it, so the first-child-
error arbitration reads the same slot it always did (D-136 untouched — the
value is still EVALUATED before the defers). Lifted: `channel()` inside a
LOOP (per-iteration reclaim; `chan_loop.npk` proves it by the first
iteration's endpoint answering `StaleHandle` seven reclaims later) and
`shared_arena` teardown (`TY_SHARED_ARENA` drops for real and left two
walker excuse tables). Riders: `exit` runs joins and defers and NO reclaims
(the drain runs generated drop bodies — the walk D-183's amendment keeps off
the controlled-shutdown path), and `.destroy()` on either arena kind clears
the binding's drop flag, the same mechanism `move` uses. The `dyn`-element
channel refusal is PERMANENT by D-207 and its rung now says so. Owning made
`shared_arena` MOVE-ONLY (TYPE-046 keys on `type_drops`), so a borrow became
the only way to share one — and **D-180 was amended (user-ratified) to make
`shared_arena<T>->` the fifth sanctioned spawn crossing**: its hazard test is
"a mutation the holder cannot see", and a shared arena has no mutation at all
(D-154 writes a slot once, before its handle escapes, and never again), while
the joined-before-freed order this cycle built is what bounds the borrow.
Two finds, neither by a test of the thing that broke: the **D-151 leak check
runs only on `exit 0`** — a program reporting success as 42 checks nothing,
which is how the first `shared_arena_drop.npk` passed against a build with
the drop disabled — and **every thread join had been sleeping its entire
five-second deadline since 1.1.9**. `npk_thread_join` waited on the
CHILD_CLEARTID word with `FUTEX_PRIVATE_FLAG`; the kernel's wake from
`mm_release` is a SHARED wake, which a private waiter never receives, so the
word was cleared promptly, the wake went nowhere, the wait ran to its
timeout, and the reload then reported success — right answer, five seconds
late, every time. One token; `mutex_basic` 5.00s → 0.12s, and the mandatory
deadline can once again tell "finished" from "stuck".
**1.4.5 (D-204) is COMPLETE**: the toolchain is a pinned build INPUT —
`nitpick.toml`'s `[toolchain]` carries the exact patch release (20.1.2 at the
time, 20.1.8 since D-349 on 2026-10-08; not a minor pin: a patch release can
change instruction selection) and the four
flag sets, and every `llc`/`opt`/`ld.lld` invocation is BUILT from those
lists, fifteen call sites and one authority, because a stated flag nothing
consumes is the next stale document. `check_toolchain_pin` asks the tools
themselves (not `llvm-config`, which is a -dev package the build does not
need) and refuses a mismatch loudly; `selfcheck.py` holds its FAILURE path.
A new **`repro` stage** runs the same compiler on the same absolute inputs
from a DIFFERENT working directory and requires the same bytes, then
assembles the compiler's own IR twice and compares the objects — the two
hazard classes the fixpoint cannot see, since IT compares two different
binaries (agreement, not determinism): H1, ASLR and hash-iteration order,
which has no controlling flag and can only be tested, and H9, the build
path leaking into the artifact, which is not hypothetical here because
D-179's site tables embed source paths. Both are clean, measured. Found:
`npkseed.py` never did embed its argv path — `Module` puts the path in a
`path` FIELD while `_path` is the location attribute nothing sets on a
module node, so the ModuleID was `"?"` by accident, one character from the
opposite; it is an explicit constant now.

`runtime/`; the Python seed builds nothing. D-205's rule changed meaning with
it: `src/` is no longer bounded by subset 1 but by what the SNAPSHOT can
compile, so a feature enters `src/` only after a snapshot that understands it.

shape, and `Trait.default_method(recv)` — refused even in one file — works.
**Step 3 is COMPLETE**: 268 counter loops are `for (intN:i in 0iN...b)` —
`..` is the INCLUSIVE range, `...` the exclusive one — and the 332 that stay

re-reads it**, so a loop bounded by a container's live count stays a `while`
and the spelling says which (proposed for ratification in 1.4.7.md). Five
match-shaped unwraps became `?|`/`?!`. **OWED-8 is CLOSED**: a type that
cannot be a channel element is `NITPICK-TYPE-057`, the checker's refusal at the

tail stored after the completion), reproduced 11 times in 120 under contention
and fixed at its source; the Bridge, the reactor and the optimiser were
exonerated by measurement, and D-228's width calibration is unblocked.

recommendations as written).
**Steps 2b–6 landed (2026-09-02), validated under D-228's cumulative-prefix protocol after a mid-step-6 UI freeze the recovery lost nothing to.** `range<T>` is spellable (2b, S-8/D-093); the snapshot was refreshed mid-cycle so `src/` and the library it imports may spell the flag families (3); every `open` caller crosses its `oflags`/`fmode` to the floor's word with `=> int32`, `open` itself staying `int64` — the floor is the syscall surface (4); `lib/nfs.npk` is the file-system surface — a sorted listing over `getdents64`, containment answered by OPENING not by string checks, restrictive creation defaults and the at-family (D-213's three riders), with `sys_cwd` and three named errnos in the prelude (5); and **D-236** renders every source path relative to the manifest root the driver finds by walking up from the main file, so the `selfhost` stage's new assertion measures H9 green — zero absolute site rows where 1,479 of 1,637 leaked before (6). Four full harnesses (main plus three cumulative-prefix worktrees) came back 58/58; main is byte-identical to the fully-merged `w456`, and a confirmatory harness on committed main followed. **Part D LANDED (2026-09-02): `npkg/` exists** — twelve modules of Nitpick built by the compiler under test, over the compiler's own path code, list and lexer. `npkg build` runs the README's ladder and produces a compiler BYTE-IDENTICAL to the harness's; `npkg test` builds, runs the runner self-check (§7.1, also `--selfcheck` alone), then every suite the harness runs unit for unit — 908 verdicts on the first full run, every suite count the harness's — with `--only`, `--verdicts PATH`, and `update`/`verify` refusing by name. The undefined-symbol scan reads the object's ELF64 symbol table itself (`npkg/elf.npk`) rather than spawning an unpinned `llvm-readelf`; the harness keeps spawning it and the new **`parity` stage** builds `npkg`, runs `npkg test --verdicts` from the manifest root, diffs the two verdict lists unit for unit and byte-compares `build/npkc` — every per-file harness site now records a verdict.
